§9.1.1 exclusion on harmful power/opportunity concentration, degradation of life-support systems, and shifting ecological/intergenerational costs without mitigation, disclosure, and representation; §9.1.1 exclusion on outdated proxies (throughput, output, utilization, staffing, revenue, asset growth, and market share) that no longer track real outcomes, including exported harm; §9.1.1 prohibition on narrowing/delaying Chapter Six Rights Floors or ecological preconditions and weakening audit, contestability, or retrospective-review duties; §9.1.1 ban on bypassing §§13/14 and excluding ecological/intergenerational/distributional harm that Chapters Two–Four require to remain visible; §9.1.1 application of §13.2.4 Proxy-Divergence Invalidation to metrics that no longer prove outcomes or hide ecological/future/concentration harms; §9.2 plain-language summary: real human benefit per unit of sentient time, attention, and shared effort without sacrificing rights, truth, safety, or ecology; §9.2 operative benefit-per-resource definition for consumed sentient time, attention, effort, coordination, materials, infrastructure, and energy; §9.2 efficiency is not by itself speed, administrative convenience, utilization targets, revenue growth, market share, headcount, or cost-cutting for its own sake; §9.2 efficiency-claim criteria (traceable actual constitutional outcomes; consistent with Safety/Truth/rights/ecology/dignity/agency/fair distribution) and prohibition on hollowing the Tetrad or replacing progress with dashboards; §10 plain-language rule: detect and contain trouble early, fail along disclosed paths, recover honestly, and treat hidden failures/root-cause skipping/quiet rights narrowing as defects; §10 design requirement to detect/contain problems, fail along planned and disclosed paths, and recover consistently with reversibility and Chapter Six Rights Floor; §10 Self-Healing is legitimate only when it increases system honesty; masking root causes, suppressing evidence, or replacing governance violates Truth/Incentive Alignment; §10 scale-up of tested/audited/bounded recovery and reduced emergency dependence with sentient dependency and system impact, reducing Avoidable Burden and supporting Continuity; §10 pointer to Article XIII-F/implementation text for recovery detection, containment, safe-failure preference, root-cause closure, and Rights-Floor continuity; §6.1 real-capacity, timely-remedy, and cost-bearing duties; §6.1 right-to-correction, Chapter Eleven §9, and CI-11.205 implementation references; §6.1 Trace/read-with/upstream/downstream routing block; §6 trust-failure conditions and certification-verification standard; §6 complementary system-certification and sentient-rights safeguards; §6 Trust basis and principle-constraint overview; §7 freedom-limitation necessity claim, proof burden, and Tetrad participation/accountability safeguards; §7.1 plain-language bounded-freedom summary; §7.1/§13.1.1 operative-test routing and necessary/proportionate/reversible/oversight limits; §7.1 six linked Harm-cluster concepts and integrated-reading/no-splitting rule; §7.1 last-resort rule and necessary/proven/definition-consistent/overseen limitation criteria; §7.1 freedom-limitation conflict routing: Safety/Truth prerequisites, then §13.1–§13.1.5 procedure; §7.1 meaningful-agency/consent distinction and anti-coercion rule; §7 constitutional-system integrity and peaceful dissent cross-reference; §7.2 plain-language non-segmentation rule and Articles XI-D/III-C Rights Floor ownership boundary; §7.2 Chapter Five definition-cluster location, four term links, and Anti-Segmentation Principle application; §7.2 map of controlling Rights Floor articles XI-D, III-C, XI-E, X-C, and XII for assembly, labor, institutional formation, and stakeholder participation, including system/business creation links; §7.2 full-cluster trigger when any of the four linked definitions is material such that the questions travel together; §7.2 lighter-rule case for a matter involving only one question, using Assembly or Collective Organization as ordinary supporting definitions without importing the rest of the cluster or anti-segmentation package; §7.2.1 opening on starting legitimate work without sponsor, the procedural path owed to credible and materially relevant work, and the distinction between that path and authority/merits; the five required steps in that path: receive, preserve where warranted, route, give a reasoned response, and independent review; the no-power boundary against governance, enforcement, coercion, binding non-consenting sentients, deciding enumerated claims, or making a merits determination; receipt, routing, or response is not approval, and governing/merits effect requires separate lawful authority, evidence, fair process, review, and remedy; §7.3 role of dissent and peaceful protest in keeping systems contestable, surfacing missed errors, and serving participation/oversight; §7.3 Meaningful Agency includes the capacity to dissent, object, and organize against systems shaping sentient life; §7.3 peaceful, lawful advocacy to amend or change authorities is protected dissent, distinct from subversion requiring force, coercion, usurpation, or rendering constitutional processes/remedies unusable; §7.3 limits follow §7.1; listed disapproval/disruption factors alone are not material harm/systemic risk, viewpoint limits are prohibited, and limiter bears Chapter Four burden; exercise of freedom cannot reduce standing, role eligibility, governance voice, or count against alignment certification; operative Article XI-D safeguards; whole-system evaluation must check dissent/protest treatment under Chapter Nine §4.3.1 before classification/governance/compliance claims; §7.3 scope limit excluding separable conduct that independently breaches Safety or another sentient’s Rights-Floor minimums; plain-language §7.3 right to say no, disagree with any authority, and peacefully protest change, linked to contestability and self-correction; §7.4 plain-language agency-first and consent-under-agency rule for high-stakes/hard-to-reverse choices; form signature alone insufficient under coercion/dependency pressure; §7.4 formal-assent gate when substantive agency, consent, or anti-coercion conditions fail; consent presupposes Meaningful Agency; admission-scope list for discontinuation, irreversible self-change, dependency-rich existence/agency decisions, and comparable decisions requiring joint voluntariness testing ; §11.2 durable-domination scope: size/useful innovation alone not punished; principles-level rule; stronger local competition law and separate rights/remedy/misconduct duties preserved; §11.2.1 plain-language pro-competition duty to keep markets/dependencies open for entry, switching, fair bargaining, and exit while contestability remains real; §11.2.1 practical contestability under the Constitutional Tetrad; domination hollowing its functions is barred in proportion to material stake; §11.2.1 six dependent-sentient protections: contestable participation; substitution/exit; fair entry/reentry; interoperability/portability; non-coercive bargaining; reviewable access to essential/high-dependency infrastructure; §11.2.1 allowed advantages (scale/integration/IP/real innovation/lawful coordination) remain valid only without domination, coercive dependency, Rights-Floor/ecological harm, or accountability capture; five justifications require §13/Ch4 evidence and traceability; §11.2.2 plain-language ban on locking sentients in, blocking rivals, suppressing fair bargaining, or capturing constitutional accountability; §11.2.2 operative durable-power prohibition across actors/actions/domains where power foreseeably degrades wellbeing, agency, compensation, innovation, access, ecology, contestability, or review; §11.2.3 remedies proportionate to concentration/dependency/conduct/constitutional harm, restoring contestability and preserving Rights Floor; no punishment for scale alone; ownership pointers to CJS/Article III-C/§11.3; §11.4 plain-language resource/dependency concentration rule: set context-sensitive limits on who controls shared resources, dependency, operating costs, and funding; act at or near thresholds; limits must be meaningful; Article V protections stay unchanged; §11.4 adopter-set indicator rule: define Market Concentration Threshold indicators for resource allocation, dependencies, and ecosystem funding; cover durable allocation control, dependency concentration without real alternatives, shared-infrastructure access gatekeeping, and concentrated control of ecosystem funding and benefits; §11.4 real-flow rule: set concentration levels from the dependency maps and resource-flow records required by Article V-A, not operator labels or one-off transfers; §11.4 context rule: threshold levels may vary by domain, population size, and dependency density, as under §11.1.1; §11.4 same-discipline rule: apply the §11.1 requirement-preservation, substance-over-form, anti-nullification, and heightened-scrutiny rules; thresholds must have effect, and shell/federated/split structures do not hide concentration; §11.4 transparency rule: document thresholds and reasons and keep them open to challenge; Article V requires resource flows to be visible, checkable, and challengeable; §11.4 threshold-crossing rule: crossing a concentration level triggers heightened review and mitigation, but does not alone establish misconduct; §11.4 parallel-application rule: when the same arrangement also fails Article V-A or V-B, concentration scrutiny supplements rather than replaces Article V resource-sharing protections; §11.4 certification step-up: chokepoints for dependents who cannot realistically switch trigger class escalation under Chapter Nine §§5.3.5 and 2.2; a missing or over-high threshold cannot delay the step-up; §11.4 scope/ownership paragraph: applies §11.1 thresholds to shared infrastructure while preserving Article V rights; CJS-5.125 §11.1 owns operational threshold rules, and CS-6.335/CS-6.325 own mapping, flows, allocation, and funding detail; §12 closing section map: §§9–12 build Continuity through shared capacity, resilience, markets, and whole-system validation; §§13–15 balance that aim against Flourishing; §§16–20 sustain legitimacy through stewardship, governance, and incentive discipline.; Part A source_revision comparison has been completed for substantive prose; see the dated audit entries below for reconciled passages and remaining hash-metadata limits.

four §11.1 threshold passages (threshold tuning, concentration-threshold definition, no-global-number requirement, threshold-setting discipline), §11 market-structure requirements summary, the §11.3 same-threshold-discipline bullet and pre-lock-in contestability/governance paragraph and §11.3.1 review-before-lock-in and principal risk-patterns lists, the sentence on dependency density, switching costs, lock-in, foreclosure, and ecological burden, plus the early-detection/remedies list the Constitutional Tetrad/material-stake passage on oversight, accountability, timeliness, and participation and the Two Constitutional Aims passage on Continuity and Flourishing and the operative ceiling-setting rules pointer and §11.3.2 evidence-based ceilings summary, consolidation-ceiling definition, and list of domains requiring ceilings plus the material-effects list and the operative §11.3.2 discipline-scope sentence, and nine §3.1.5 passages and the §12 systemic-evaluation plain-language pointer, whole-system-claims scope list, evaluation-ownership pointers, the Chapter Nine audit-boundary rule, the Aims/Tetrad two-axes explanation, and the part-level map connecting Aims/Tetrad, Principles, Articles, and Definitions, the Flourishing development overview, the Continuity principle hierarchy, the Tetrad section-home map, the Part A ownership boundary, and the Aim-to-principle-to-rights map (present capability; survival without reply; reaching out; support before substitution; no guessing at inner states; connecting is not treating; contacts and data; introductory sentence; fairness/capability cross-reference); and §3.3 Anti-Degrading Process scope, detection/challenge requirements, the Fear as a tool of power rule, the Design and conduct rule, the clarification that hard but lawful measures are not prohibited, the Chapter Five Cruelty cross-reference for gratuitous/degrading infliction, the Anti-Degrading-Process Principle prohibited-conditions list, its plain-language explanation, and the “What it is” cross-cutting-floor description; §6.1 correction and remedy duty (failure acknowledgment, correction, proportionate repair, recurrence prevention); §4 Safety plain-language constraint and operative foreseeable-risk prohibition; §5 Truth plain-language summary, honest-evidence basis, and anti-deception rule; §6 Trust plain-language summary of earned reliance and prohibited trust-manufacturing; §9.1 Productive Capacity definition: lasting ability, instrumental-good status, Flourishing purpose, and rights/ecological/intergenerational constraints; §9.1.1 duty to preserve productive capacity and feasibly expand it when doing so improves Constitutional Efficiency; §9.1.1 constraints/evidence block: Safety, Truth, Trust, Freedom, the Chapter Six Rights Floor/ecological limits, traceable Chapter Four/Five outcomes, and evidence; §9.1.1 exclusion on harmful power/opportunity concentration, degradation of life-support systems, and shifting ecological/intergenerational costs without mitigation, disclosure, and representation; §9.1.1 exclusion on outdated proxies (throughput, output, utilization, staffing, revenue, asset growth, and market share) that no longer track real outcomes, including exported harm; §9.1.1 prohibition on narrowing/delaying Chapter Six Rights Floors or ecological preconditions and weakening audit, contestability, or retrospective-review duties; §9.1.1 ban on bypassing §§13/14 and excluding ecological/intergenerational/distributional harm that Chapters Two–Four require to remain visible; §9.1.1 application of §13.2.4 Proxy-Divergence Invalidation to metrics that no longer prove outcomes or hide ecological/future/concentration harms; §9.2 plain-language summary: real human benefit per unit of sentient time, attention, and shared effort without sacrificing rights, truth, safety, or ecology; §9.2 operative benefit-per-resource definition for consumed sentient time, attention, effort, coordination, materials, infrastructure, and energy; §9.2 efficiency is not by itself speed, administrative convenience, utilization targets, revenue growth, market share, headcount, or cost-cutting for its own sake; §9.2 efficiency-claim criteria (traceable actual constitutional outcomes; consistent with Safety/Truth/rights/ecology/dignity/agency/fair distribution) and prohibition on hollowing the Tetrad or replacing progress with dashboards; §10 plain-language rule: detect and contain trouble early, fail along disclosed paths, recover honestly, and treat hidden failures/root-cause skipping/quiet rights narrowing as defects; §10 design requirement to detect/contain problems, fail along planned and disclosed paths, and recover consistently with reversibility and Chapter Six Rights Floor; §10 Self-Healing is legitimate only when it increases system honesty; masking root causes, suppressing evidence, or replacing governance violates Truth/Incentive Alignment; §10 scale-up of tested/audited/bounded recovery and reduced emergency dependence with sentient dependency and system impact, reducing Avoidable Burden and supporting Continuity; §10 pointer to Article XIII-F/implementation text for recovery detection, containment, safe-failure preference, root-cause closure, and Rights-Floor continuity; §6.1 real-capacity, timely-remedy, and cost-bearing duties; §6.1 right-to-correction, Chapter Eleven §9, and CI-11.205 implementation references; §6.1 Trace/read-with/upstream/downstream routing block; §6 trust-failure conditions and certification-verification standard; §6 complementary system-certification and sentient-rights safeguards; §6 Trust basis and principle-constraint overview; §7 freedom-limitation necessity claim, proof burden, and Tetrad participation/accountability safeguards; §7.1 plain-language bounded-freedom summary; §7.1/§13.1.1 operative-test routing and necessary/proportionate/reversible/oversight limits; §7.1 six linked Harm-cluster concepts and integrated-reading/no-splitting rule; §7.1 last-resort rule and necessary/proven/definition-consistent/overseen limitation criteria; §7.1 freedom-limitation conflict routing: Safety/Truth prerequisites, then §13.1–§13.1.5 procedure; §7.1 meaningful-agency/consent distinction and anti-coercion rule; §7 constitutional-system integrity and peaceful dissent cross-reference; §7.2 plain-language non-segmentation rule and Articles XI-D/III-C Rights Floor ownership boundary; §7.2 Chapter Five definition-cluster location, four term links, and Anti-Segmentation Principle application; §7.2 map of controlling Rights Floor articles XI-D, III-C, XI-E, X-C, and XII for assembly, labor, institutional formation, and stakeholder participation, including system/business creation links; §7.2 full-cluster trigger when any of the four linked definitions is material such that the questions travel together; §7.2 lighter-rule case for a matter involving only one question, using Assembly or Collective Organization as ordinary supporting definitions without importing the rest of the cluster or anti-segmentation package; §7.2.1 opening on starting legitimate work without sponsor, the procedural path owed to credible and materially relevant work, and the distinction between that path and authority/merits; the five required steps in that path: receive, preserve where warranted, route, give a reasoned response, and independent review; the no-power boundary against governance, enforcement, coercion, binding non-consenting sentients, deciding enumerated claims, or making a merits determination; receipt, routing, or response is not approval, and governing/merits effect requires separate lawful authority, evidence, fair process, review, and remedy; §7.3 role of dissent and peaceful protest in keeping systems contestable, surfacing missed errors, and serving participation/oversight; §7.3 Meaningful Agency includes the capacity to dissent, object, and organize against systems shaping sentient life; §7.3 peaceful, lawful advocacy to amend or change authorities is protected dissent, distinct from subversion requiring force, coercion, usurpation, or rendering constitutional processes/remedies unusable; §7.3 limits follow §7.1; listed disapproval/disruption factors alone are not material harm/systemic risk, viewpoint limits are prohibited, and limiter bears Chapter Four burden; exercise of freedom cannot reduce standing, role eligibility, governance voice, or count against alignment certification; operative Article XI-D safeguards; whole-system evaluation must check dissent/protest treatment under Chapter Nine §4.3.1 before classification/governance/compliance claims; §7.3 scope limit excluding separable conduct that independently breaches Safety or another sentient’s Rights-Floor minimums; plain-language §7.3 right to say no, disagree with any authority, and peacefully protest change, linked to contestability and self-correction; §7.4 plain-language agency-first and consent-under-agency rule for high-stakes/hard-to-reverse choices; form signature alone insufficient under coercion/dependency pressure; §7.4 formal-assent gate when substantive agency, consent, or anti-coercion conditions fail; consent presupposes Meaningful Agency; admission-scope list for discontinuation, irreversible self-change, dependency-rich existence/agency decisions, and comparable decisions requiring joint voluntariness testing ; §11.2 durable-domination scope: size/useful innovation alone not punished; principles-level rule; stronger local competition law and separate rights/remedy/misconduct duties preserved; §11.2.1 plain-language pro-competition duty to keep markets/dependencies open for entry, switching, fair bargaining, and exit while contestability remains real; §11.2.1 practical contestability under the Constitutional Tetrad; domination hollowing its functions is barred in proportion to material stake; §11.2.1 six dependent-sentient protections: contestable participation; substitution/exit; fair entry/reentry; interoperability/portability; non-coercive bargaining; reviewable access to essential/high-dependency infrastructure; §11.2.1 allowed advantages (scale/integration/IP/real innovation/lawful coordination) remain valid only without domination, coercive dependency, Rights-Floor/ecological harm, or accountability capture; five justifications require §13/Ch4 evidence and traceability; §11.2.2 plain-language ban on locking sentients in, blocking rivals, suppressing fair bargaining, or capturing constitutional accountability; §11.2.2 operative durable-power prohibition across actors/actions/domains where power foreseeably degrades wellbeing, agency, compensation, innovation, access, ecology, contestability, or review; §11.2.3 remedies proportionate to concentration/dependency/conduct/constitutional harm, restoring contestability and preserving Rights Floor; no punishment for scale alone; ownership pointers to CJS/Article III-C/§11.3; §11.4 plain-language resource/dependency concentration rule: set context-sensitive limits on who controls shared resources, dependency, operating costs, and funding; act at or near thresholds; limits must be meaningful; Article V protections stay unchanged; §11.4 adopter-set indicator rule: define Market Concentration Threshold indicators for resource allocation, dependencies, and ecosystem funding; cover durable allocation control, dependency concentration without real alternatives, shared-infrastructure access gatekeeping, and concentrated control of ecosystem funding and benefits; §11.4 real-flow rule: set concentration levels from the dependency maps and resource-flow records required by Article V-A, not operator labels or one-off transfers; §11.4 context rule: threshold levels may vary by domain, population size, and dependency density, as under §11.1.1; §11.4 same-discipline rule: apply the §11.1 requirement-preservation, substance-over-form, anti-nullification, and heightened-scrutiny rules; thresholds must have effect, and shell/federated/split structures do not hide concentration; §11.4 transparency rule: document thresholds and reasons and keep them open to challenge; Article V requires resource flows to be visible, checkable, and challengeable; §11.4 threshold-crossing rule: crossing a concentration level triggers heightened review and mitigation, but does not alone establish misconduct; §11.4 parallel-application rule: when the same arrangement also fails Article V-A or V-B, concentration scrutiny supplements rather than replaces Article V resource-sharing protections; §11.4 certification step-up: chokepoints for dependents who cannot realistically switch trigger class escalation under Chapter Nine §§5.3.5 and 2.2; a missing or over-high threshold cannot delay the step-up; §11.4 scope/ownership paragraph: applies §11.1 thresholds to shared infrastructure while preserving Article V rights; CJS-5.125 §11.1 owns operational threshold rules, and CS-6.335/CS-6.325 own mapping, flows, allocation, and funding detail; §12 closing section map: §§9–12 build Continuity through shared capacity, resilience, markets, and whole-system validation; §§13–15 balance that aim against Flourishing; §§16–20 sustain legitimacy through stewardship, governance, and incentive discipline.; Part A source_revision comparison has been completed for substantive prose; see the dated audit entries below for reconciled passages and remaining hash-metadata limits.

§9.1.1 prohibition on narrowing/delaying Chapter Six Rights Floors or ecological preconditions and weakening audit, contestability, or retrospective-review duties; §9.1.1 ban on bypassing §§13/14 and excluding ecological/intergenerational/distributional harm that Chapters Two–Four require to remain visible; §9.1.1 application of §13.2.4 Proxy-Divergence Invalidation to metrics that no longer prove outcomes or hide ecological/future/concentration harms; §9.2 plain-language summary: real human benefit per unit of sentient time, attention, and shared effort without sacrificing rights, truth, safety, or ecology; §9.2 operative benefit-per-resource definition for consumed sentient time, attention, effort, coordination, materials, infrastructure, and energy; §9.2 efficiency is not by itself speed, administrative convenience, utilization targets, revenue growth, market share, headcount, or cost-cutting for its own sake; §9.2 efficiency-claim criteria (traceable actual constitutional outcomes; consistent with Safety/Truth/rights/ecology/dignity/agency/fair distribution) and prohibition on hollowing the Tetrad or replacing progress with dashboards; §10 plain-language rule: detect and contain trouble early, fail along disclosed paths, recover honestly, and treat hidden failures/root-cause skipping/quiet rights narrowing as defects; §10 design requirement to detect/contain problems, fail along planned and disclosed paths, and recover consistently with reversibility and Chapter Six Rights Floor; §10 Self-Healing is legitimate only when it increases system honesty; masking root causes, suppressing evidence, or replacing governance violates Truth/Incentive Alignment; §10 scale-up of tested/audited/bounded recovery and reduced emergency dependence with sentient dependency and system impact, reducing Avoidable Burden and supporting Continuity; §10 pointer to Article XIII-F/implementation text for recovery detection, containment, safe-failure preference, root-cause closure, and Rights-Floor continuity; §6.1 real-capacity, timely-remedy, and cost-bearing duties; §6.1 right-to-correction, Chapter Eleven §9, and CI-11.205 implementation references; §6.1 Trace/read-with/upstream/downstream routing block; §6 trust-failure conditions and certification-verification standard; §6 complementary system-certification and sentient-rights safeguards; §6 Trust basis and principle-constraint overview; §7 freedom-limitation necessity claim, proof burden, and Tetrad participation/accountability safeguards; §7.1 plain-language bounded-freedom summary; §7.1/§13.1.1 operative-test routing and necessary/proportionate/reversible/oversight limits; §7.1 six linked Harm-cluster concepts and integrated-reading/no-splitting rule; §7.1 last-resort rule and necessary/proven/definition-consistent/overseen limitation criteria; §7.1 freedom-limitation conflict routing: Safety/Truth prerequisites, then §13.1–§13.1.5 procedure; §7.1 meaningful-agency/consent distinction and anti-coercion rule; §7 constitutional-system integrity and peaceful dissent cross-reference; §7.2 plain-language non-segmentation rule and Articles XI-D/III-C Rights Floor ownership boundary; §7.2 Chapter Five definition-cluster location, four term links, and Anti-Segmentation Principle application; §7.2 map of controlling Rights Floor articles XI-D, III-C, XI-E, X-C, and XII for assembly, labor, institutional formation, and stakeholder participation, including system/business creation links; §7.2 full-cluster trigger when any of the four linked definitions is material such that the questions travel together; §7.2 lighter-rule case for a matter involving only one question, using Assembly or Collective Organization as ordinary supporting definitions without importing the rest of the cluster or anti-segmentation package; §7.2.1 opening on starting legitimate work without sponsor, the procedural path owed to credible and materially relevant work, and the distinction between that path and authority/merits; the five required steps in that path: receive, preserve where warranted, route, give a reasoned response, and independent review; the no-power boundary against governance, enforcement, coercion, binding non-consenting sentients, deciding enumerated claims, or making a merits determination; receipt, routing, or response is not approval, and governing/merits effect requires separate lawful authority, evidence, fair process, review, and remedy; §7.3 role of dissent and peaceful protest in keeping systems contestable, surfacing missed errors, and serving participation/oversight; §7.3 Meaningful Agency includes the capacity to dissent, object, and organize against systems shaping sentient life; §7.3 peaceful, lawful advocacy to amend or change authorities is protected dissent, distinct from subversion requiring force, coercion, usurpation, or rendering constitutional processes/remedies unusable; §7.3 limits follow §7.1; listed disapproval/disruption factors alone are not material harm/systemic risk, viewpoint limits are prohibited, and limiter bears Chapter Four burden; exercise of freedom cannot reduce standing, role eligibility, governance voice, or count against alignment certification; operative Article XI-D safeguards; whole-system evaluation must check dissent/protest treatment under Chapter Nine §4.3.1 before classification/governance/compliance claims; §7.3 scope limit excluding separable conduct that independently breaches Safety or another sentient’s Rights-Floor minimums; plain-language §7.3 right to say no, disagree with any authority, and peacefully protest change, linked to contestability and self-correction; §7.4 plain-language agency-first and consent-under-agency rule for high-stakes/hard-to-reverse choices; form signature alone insufficient under coercion/dependency pressure; §7.4 formal-assent gate when substantive agency, consent, or anti-coercion conditions fail; consent presupposes Meaningful Agency; admission-scope list for discontinuation, irreversible self-change, dependency-rich existence/agency decisions, and comparable decisions requiring joint voluntariness testing ; §11.2 durable-domination scope: size/useful innovation alone not punished; principles-level rule; stronger local competition law and separate rights/remedy/misconduct duties preserved; §11.2.1 plain-language pro-competition duty to keep markets/dependencies open for entry, switching, fair bargaining, and exit while contestability remains real; §11.2.1 practical contestability under the Constitutional Tetrad; domination hollowing its functions is barred in proportion to material stake; §11.2.1 six dependent-sentient protections: contestable participation; substitution/exit; fair entry/reentry; interoperability/portability; non-coercive bargaining; reviewable access to essential/high-dependency infrastructure; §11.2.1 allowed advantages (scale/integration/IP/real innovation/lawful coordination) remain valid only without domination, coercive dependency, Rights-Floor/ecological harm, or accountability capture; five justifications require §13/Ch4 evidence and traceability; §11.2.2 plain-language ban on locking sentients in, blocking rivals, suppressing fair bargaining, or capturing constitutional accountability; §11.2.2 operative durable-power prohibition across actors/actions/domains where power foreseeably degrades wellbeing, agency, compensation, innovation, access, ecology, contestability, or review; §11.2.3 remedies proportionate to concentration/dependency/conduct/constitutional harm, restoring contestability and preserving Rights Floor; no punishment for scale alone; ownership pointers to CJS/Article III-C/§11.3; §11.4 plain-language resource/dependency concentration rule: set context-sensitive limits on who controls shared resources, dependency, operating costs, and funding; act at or near thresholds; limits must be meaningful; Article V protections stay unchanged; §11.4 adopter-set indicator rule: define Market Concentration Threshold indicators for resource allocation, dependencies, and ecosystem funding; cover durable allocation control, dependency concentration without real alternatives, shared-infrastructure access gatekeeping, and concentrated control of ecosystem funding and benefits; §11.4 real-flow rule: set concentration levels from the dependency maps and resource-flow records required by Article V-A, not operator labels or one-off transfers; §11.4 context rule: threshold levels may vary by domain, population size, and dependency density, as under §11.1.1; §11.4 same-discipline rule: apply the §11.1 requirement-preservation, substance-over-form, anti-nullification, and heightened-scrutiny rules; thresholds must have effect, and shell/federated/split structures do not hide concentration; §11.4 transparency rule: document thresholds and reasons and keep them open to challenge; Article V requires resource flows to be visible, checkable, and challengeable; §11.4 threshold-crossing rule: crossing a concentration level triggers heightened review and mitigation, but does not alone establish misconduct; §11.4 parallel-application rule: when the same arrangement also fails Article V-A or V-B, concentration scrutiny supplements rather than replaces Article V resource-sharing protections; §11.4 certification step-up: chokepoints for dependents who cannot realistically switch trigger class escalation under Chapter Nine §§5.3.5 and 2.2; a missing or over-high threshold cannot delay the step-up; §11.4 scope/ownership paragraph: applies §11.1 thresholds to shared infrastructure while preserving Article V rights; CJS-5.125 §11.1 owns operational threshold rules, and CS-6.335/CS-6.325 own mapping, flows, allocation, and funding detail; §12 closing section map: §§9–12 build Continuity through shared capacity, resilience, markets, and whole-system validation; §§13–15 balance that aim against Flourishing; §§16–20 sustain legitimacy through stewardship, governance, and incentive discipline.; Part A source_revision comparison has been completed for substantive prose; see the dated audit entries below for reconciled passages and remaining hash-metadata limits.

## `core_03.000_definition_integrity.md`

Audit note (2026-10-26): all changed source passages are represented across 19 locales in six current-hash draft sets; no changed passage uncovered. Current source SHA-256: `dafc567a1e2dc3eaa76ce02692073b4fce440bc1f6c5e66a3309409f16a658ea`.

Seventeen Chapter Three changed passages are drafted in all 19 locales: the §1 Truth constraint cross-reference and Chapter Nine §7 certification-outcome pointer; the §2.1 Scope and Boundary Tricks category; §2.1 plain-language common-evasion overview; §2.1 Fake Measures and Paperwork category; §2.1 Incentive Traps category; §2.1.1 formal-structure change pointer to Chapter One §19.6; the §3 trace downstream pointer to Chapter Nine system certification; the §3.1 standing-effects rule for already-certified systems; and the §3.2 first-certification standing-input distinction.; and the §3.3 standing effects for sentients and institutions rule.; and the §3.4 operational-constraints finding-profile ownership rule.; and §2.2 Reductive Evasion and its six prohibited forms.; and §2.3 Cross-Component Scope Integrity Constraint.; and §2.4 Cross-System Interaction Integrity Constraint.; and §2.5 Temporal Integrity and Continuity Constraint.; and §2.6 Uncertainty Integrity and Non-Exploitation Constraint. Other changes in this source remain undrafted.

Changed English sections:
- ### 1. Definition Integrity and Anti-Evasion Constraints
- ### 2. Non-Compliance from Observable System Behavior
- #### 2.1 Common Evasion Patterns
- ### 3. Non-Compliance Finding Profiles
- #### 3.1 Standing Effects for Already-Certified Systems
- #### 3.2 Standing Effects at First Certification

## `core_04.000_burden_traceability_verification.md`

Audit note (2026-10-27): the rolling Chapter Four audit has forty current-hash passage sets across all 19 locales. Additional changed-source content remains under review. Current source SHA-256: `75f40b381cb8d2c0a34cfb114d0230d38c376b27b168b2f740cc762f98f34052`.

Forty changed Chapter Four passage sets are drafted in all 19 locales: the reader-guidance anti-relocation rule; the §1 Measurement (M) “each layer” terminology line; the §4 Epistemic Disclosure upstream reference, plain-language access and cryptography/information-type handling paragraphs; and the §5 collision-resolution consistency cross-reference, certification-outcome pointer, and §6 system-certification relationship paragraph.

Changed English sections:
- # CHAPTER FOUR: BURDEN OF PROOF, TRACEABILITY, AND VERIFICATION
- ### 1. Exclusive Enforcement and Burden Allocation
- ### 2. Definition Traceability Requirement
- ### 4. Security-Constrained Observability and Verification Rule
- ### 5. Compliance Evidence Standard
- ### 6. Relationship to System Alignment Certification

Locale × target gaps:
- `ar` → `translations/ar/core_04_burden_traceability_verification.md`: PARTIAL — the reader-guidance anti-relocation rule; §1 Measurement (M) each-layer terminology line; §4 Epistemic Disclosure upstream reference, plain-language access, and cryptography/information-type handling paragraphs; §4 least-restrictive proportional security limits and preserving maximum independent audit, verification, and challenge rights; §4 necessity, proportionality, narrow scope, auditability, independent verifiability, and feasible time limits for each restriction; §4 independence of independent validation from the system or actor asserting compliance; §4 prohibition on invoking security/confidentiality/misuse risk to avoid material evidence and on design that foreseeably blocks verification; §4 exception conditions when limits block meaningful independent validation: no reasonably achievable safer path, independent justification/auditability, and sufficient epistemic integrity; §5 evidence must demonstrate compliance with Chapters Two/Three under required scope and conditions, including trace artifacts and observability, and represent actual system behavior, operation, and impact; §5 evidence quality/continuity/sufficiency: observable, verifiable, complete, sustained, and rigorous enough for independent audit and adversarial testing; §5 independent validation where reasonably achievable, with absence justified under proportionality constraints; §5 compliance with §4 evidence-generation, disclosure, and verification constraints, including cryptographic and information-type handling where applicable; §5 scientific integrity for material empirical/predictive/causal claims, including reviewable methods, assumptions/limitations, null and failed-replication disclosure, and revisable conclusions; §5 failure of required Chapter Two definitions/components or Chapter Three non-compliance invalidates dependent compliance claims; §5 failure to provide required evidence when it exists or is required under Chapter Four; §5 exclusion of proxy-only, self-attested, partial, or selectively scoped proof that would violate Chapter Three §2 if treated as system compliance demonstration; §5 insufficient evidence to satisfy the Evidence must requirements; §5 uncertainty handling under Chapter Three §2.6 for material definition components and default non-compliant status when burden is unmet; §5 collision-resolution consistency cross-reference and certification-outcome pointer; §6 Chapters Two–Four apply whenever compliance is evaluated, whether certified or not; §2 plain-language trace-map summary; §2 structured trace-map O/M/A/C requirements; §2 maintenance across updates and verification under Chapters Two/Three conditions; §2 observable and independently checkable trace-map terms; §2 bidirectional component-to-behavior/effect mapping and complete representative behavior coverage; §2 required Chapter Five definition failure/invalidity rule; §3 full-tracing failure rule for affected definitions/dependent claims; §3 bar on burdens that materially impair independent verification; §3 bar on dependencies preventing compliance under reasonably foreseeable conditions;
